Boundary values never change (they are copied from\n// input to output every iteration), so both buffers are initialised with the same\n// boundary once and only the interior is written here.\n__global__ void __launch_bounds__(BLOCK_X * BLOCK_Y)\nstencilIterationKernel(const Real* __restrict__ input,\n                       Real* __restrict__ output,\n                       const size_t nx, const size_t ny, const size_t nz) {\n    const size_t x = (size_t)blockIdx.x * BLOCK_X + threadIdx.x + 1;\n    const size_t y = (size_t)blockIdx.y * BLOCK_Y + threadIdx.y + 1;\n    if (x >= nx - 1 || y >= ny - 1) return;\n\n    const size_t zBegin = (size_t)blockIdx.z * Z_CHUNK + 1;\n    if (zBegin >= nz - 1) return;\n    const size_t zEnd = zBegin + Z_CHUNK < nz - 1 ? zBegin + Z_CHUNK : nz - 1;\n\n    const size_t plane = nx * ny;\n    size_t idx = idx3(x, y, zBegin, nx, ny);\n\n    Real bottom = __ldg(&input[idx - plane]);\n    Real center = __ldg(&input[idx]);\n    for (size_t z = zBegin; z < zEnd; ++z) {\n        const Real top = __ldg(&input[idx + plane]);\n        const Real left = __ldg(&input[idx - 1]);\n        const Real right = __ldg(&input[idx + 1]);\n        const Real front = __ldg(&input[idx - nx]);\n        const Real back = __ldg(&input[idx + nx]);\n\n        // Simple averaging stencil (same summation order as the reference)\n        output[idx] = (center + left + right + front + back + bottom + top) / 7.0;\n\n        bottom = center;\n        center = top;\n        idx += plane;\n    }\n}\n\n'''\nsrc = src[:start] + cuda_code + src[end:]\n\n# idx3 must be usable on device\nsrc = src.replace('inline constexpr size_t idx3', '__host__ __device__ inline constexpr size_t idx3')\n\n# main body: allocation through stencil loop\nold_start = src.index('    // Allocate grids (double buffering)')\nold_end = src.index('    printf(\"Computation time')\nnew_main = r'''    // Device grids (double buffering)\n    Real* d_grid1 = nullptr;\n    Real* d_grid2 = nullptr;\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaMalloc(&d_grid1, gridSize * sizeof(Real)));\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaMalloc(&d_grid2, gridSize * sizeof(Real)));\n\n    // Initialize\n    printf(\"Initializing grid...\\n\");\n    {\n        int device = 0, numSMs = 0;\n        CUDA_CHECK(cudaGetDevice(&device));\n        CUDA_CHECK(cudaDeviceGetAttribute(&numSMs, cudaDevAttrMultiProcessorCount, device));\n        const int threads = 256;\n        size_t blocks = (gridSize + threads - 1) / threads;\n        blocks = std::min<size_t>(blocks, (size_t)numSMs * 32);\n        if (blocks > 0) {\n            initializeGridKernel<<<(unsigned)blocks, threads>>>(d_grid1, gridSize);\n            CUDA_CHECK(cudaGetLastError());\n            // Both buffers share the (never changing) boundary values.\n            CUDA_CHECK(cudaMemcpy(d_grid2, d_grid1, gridSize * sizeof(Real), cudaMemcpyDeviceToDevice));\n        }\n        CUDA_CHECK(cudaDeviceSynchronize());\n    }\n\n    const bool hasInterior = nx > 2 && ny > 2 && nz > 2;\n    const dim3 block(BLOCK_X, BLOCK_Y, 1);\n    const dim3 grid(hasInterior ? (unsigned)((nx - 2 + BLOCK_X - 1) / BLOCK_X) : 0,\n                    hasInterior ? (unsigned)((ny - 2 + BLOCK_Y - 1) / BLOCK_Y) : 0,\n                    hasInterior ? 1;\n\n    const size_t plane = nx * ny;\n    size_t idx = idx3(x, y, zBegin, nx, ny);\n\n    Real bottom = __ldg(&input[idx - plane]);\n    Real center = __ldg(&input[idx]);\n    for (size_t z = zBegin; z < zEnd; ++z) {\n        const Real top = __ldg(&input[idx + plane]);\n        const Real left = __ldg(&input[idx - 1]);\n        const Real right = __ldg(&input[idx + 1]);\n        const Real front = __ldg(&input[idx - nx]);\n        const Real back = __ldg(&input[idx + nx]);\n\n        // Simple averaging stencil (same summation order as the reference)\n        output[idx] = (center + left + right + front + back + bottom + top) / 7.0;\n\n        bottom = center;\n        center = top;\n        idx += plane;\n    }\n}\n\n'''\nsrc = src[:start] + cuda_code + src[end:]\n\n# idx3 must be usable on device\nsrc = src.replace('inline constexpr size_t idx3', '__host__ __device__ inline constexpr size_t idx3')\n\n# main body: allocation through stencil loop\nold_start = src.index('    // Allocate grids (double buffering)')\nold_end = src.index('    printf(\"Computation time')\nnew_main = r'''    // Device grids (double buffering)\n    Real* d_grid1 = nullptr;\n    Real* d_grid2 = nullptr;\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaMalloc(&d_grid1, gridSize * sizeof(Real)));\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaMalloc(&d_grid2, gridSize * sizeof(Real)));\n\n    // Initialize\n    printf(\"Initializing grid...\\n\");\n    {\n        int device = 0, numSMs = 0;\n        CUDA_CHECK(cudaGetDevice(&device));\n        CUDA_CHECK(cudaDeviceGetAttribute(&numSMs, cudaDevAttrMultiProcessorCount, device));\n        const int threads = 256;\n        size_t blocks = (gridSize + threads - 1) / threads;\n        blocks = std::min<size_t>(blocks, (size_t)numSMs * 32);\n        if (blocks > 0) {\n            initializeGridKernel<<<(unsigned)blocks, threads>>>(d_grid1, gridSize);\n            CUDA_CHECK(cudaGetLastError());\n            // Both buffers share the (never changing) boundary values.\n            CUDA_CHECK(cudaMemcpy(d_grid2, d_grid1, gridSize * sizeof(Real), cudaMemcpyDeviceToDevice));\n        }\n        CUDA_CHECK(cudaDeviceSynchronize());\n    }\n\n    const bool hasInterior = nx > 2 && ny > 2 && nz > 2;\n    const dim3 block(BLOCK_X, BLOCK_Y, 1);\n    const dim3 grid(hasInterior ? (unsigned)((nx - 2 + BLOCK_X - 1) / BLOCK_X) : 0,\n                    hasInterior ? (unsigned)((ny - 2 + BLOCK_Y - 1) / BLOCK_Y) : 0,\n                    hasInterior ? (unsigned)((nz - 2 + Z_CHUNK - 1) / Z_CHUNK) : 0);\n\n    // Run stencil iterations\n    printf(\"Running stencil computation...\\n\");\n    auto start = std::chrono::high_resolution_clock::now();\n\n    if (hasInterior) {\n        for (int iter = 0; iter < iterations; ++iter) {\n            if (iter % 2 == 0) {\n                stencilIterationKernel<<<grid, block>>>(d_grid1, d_grid2, nx, ny, nz);\n            } else {\n                stencilIterationKernel<<<grid, block>>>(d_grid2, d_grid1, nx, ny, nz);\n            }\n        }\n        CUDA_CHECK(cudaGetLastError());\n    }\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaDeviceSynchronize());\n\n    auto end = std::chrono::high_resolution_clock::now();\n    auto duration = std::chrono::duration_cast<std::chrono::milliseconds>(end - start);\n\n'''\nsrc = src[:old_start] + new_main + src[old_end:]\n\nsrc = src.replace('''    const std::vector<Real>& finalGrid = (iterations % 2 == 0) ? grid1 : grid2;\n''', '''    std::vector<Real> finalGrid(gridSize);\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaMemcpy(finalGrid.data(), (iterations % 2 == 0) ? d_grid1 : d_grid2,\n                          gridSize * sizeof(Real), cudaMemcpyDeviceToHost));\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaFree(d_grid1));\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaFree(d_grid2));\n''')\nopen('stencil3d.cpp','w').write(src)\nEOF\ncat > CMakeLists.txt <<'EOF'\ncmake_minimum_required(VERSION 3.18)\nproject(stencil3d_benchmark LANGUAGES CXX CUDA)\n\nset(CMAKE_CXX_STANDARD 20)\nset(CMAKE_CXX_STANDARD_REQUIRED ON)\nset(CMAKE_CUDA_STANDARD 20)\nset(CMAKE_CUDA_STANDARD_REQUIRED ON)\n\nif(NOT DEFINED CMAKE_CUDA_ARCHITECTURES)\n  set(CMAKE_CUDA_ARCHITECTURES native)\nendif()\n\n# Compile the source as CUDA\nset_source_files_properties(stencil3d.cpp PROPERTIES LANGUAGE CUDA)\nadd_executable(stencil3d stencil3d.cpp)\n\n# Enable optimizations (no fast-math / FMA contraction changes: keep results bit-identical)\ntarget_compile_options(stencil3d PRIVATE\n  $<$<COMPILE_LANGUAGE:CUDA>:-O3 -Xcompiler=-O3,-march=native,-Wall,-Wextra>)\n\n# Link math library\ntarget_link_libraries(stencil3d m)\nEOF\nrm -rf build && cmake -S . -B build -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Release >/dev/null && cmake --build build 2>&1 | grep -E \"error|warning|Built\" ; ./build/stencil3d -x 256 -i 20 -r | tail -14","uuid":"66ee4bbd-82ea-46d8-aa5c-5ee77fcfc971","session_id":"ca67c419-17df-453f-8ff0-f89b287415ee"}

FP64 throughput is the\n// bottleneck on consumer GPUs and this is much cheaper than the generic division.\n__device__ __forceinline__ Real div7(const Real s) {\n    constexpr Real inv7 = 1.0 / 7.0;\n    const Real q = s * inv7;\n    const Real r = fma(-q, 7.0, s);\n    return fma(r, inv7, q);\n}\n\n// 7-point stencil computation over interior points.\n// Each thread owns one (x, y) column and marches along a chunk of z, keeping the\n// z-neighbours in registers. Boundary values never change (they are copied from\n// input to output every iteration), so both buffers are initialised with the same\n// boundary once and only the interior is written here.\n__global__ void __launch_bounds__(BLOCK_X * BLOCK_Y)\nstencilIterationKernel(const Real* __restrict__ input,\n                       Real* __restrict__ output,\n                       const size_t nx, const size_t ny, const size_t nz) {\n    const size_t x = (size_t)blockIdx.x * BLOCK_X + threadIdx.x + 1;\n    const size_t y = (size_t)blockIdx.y * BLOCK_Y + threadIdx.y + 1;\n    if (x >= nx - 1 || y >= ny - 1) return;\n\n    const size_t zBegin = (size_t)blockIdx.z * Z_CHUNK + 1;\n    if (zBegin >= nz - 1) return;\n    const size_t zEnd = zBegin + Z_CHUNK < nz - 1 ? zBegin + Z_CHUNK : nz - 1;\n\n    const size_t plane = nx * ny;\n    size_t idx = idx3(x, y, zBegin, nx, ny);\n\n    Real bottom = __ldg(&input[idx - plane]);\n    Real center = __ldg(&input[idx]);\n    for (size_t z = zBegin; z < zEnd; ++z) {\n        const Real top = __ldg(&input[idx + plane]);\n        const Real left = __ldg(&input[idx - 1]);\n        const Real right = __ldg(&input[idx + 1]);\n        const Real front = __ldg(&input[idx - nx]);\n        const Real back = __ldg(&input[idx + nx]);\n\n        // Simple averaging stencil (same summation order as the reference)\n        output[idx] = div7((center + left + right + front + back + bottom + top));\n\n        bottom = center;\n        center = top;\n        idx += plane;\n    }\n}\n\nbool validateResult(const std::vector<Real>& grid, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t nx, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t ny, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t nz) {\n    // Simple sanity checks\n    \n    // 1. Values should be reasonable (bounded)\n    Real minVal = grid[0];\n    Real maxVal = grid[0];\n    for (const auto& val : grid) {\n        minVal = std::min(minVal, val);\n        maxVal = std::max(maxVal, val);\n    }\n    \n    printf(\"Value range: [%.6f, %.6f]\\n\", minVal, maxVal);\n    \n    // After averaging, values should be somewhat bounded\n    if (maxVal > 1e6 || minVal < -1e6) {\n        printf(\"Validation failed: values out of expected range\\n\");\n        return false;\n    }\n    \n    // 3. Boundary values should not change significantly\n    // (they are copied, so they should be close to initial values)\n    \n    return true;\n}\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-i\") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {\n            iterations = atoi(argv[++i]);\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-v\") == 0) {\n            validate = true;\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-r\") == 0) {\n            printResults = true;\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-h\") == 0) {\n            printUsage(argv[0]);\n            return 0;\n        } else {\n            printf(\"Unknown option: %s\\n\", argv[i]);\n            printUsage(argv[0]);\n            return 1;\n        }\n    }\n    \n    if (ny == 0) ny = nx;\n    if (nz == 0) nz = nx;\n    \n    printf(\"3D Stencil Benchmark\\n\");\n    printf(\"Grid size: %zu x %zu x %zu\\n\", nx, ny, nz);\n    printf(\"Iterations: %d\\n\", iterations);\n    printf(\"Validation: %s\\n\", validate ? \"enabled\" : \"disabled\");\n    \n    size_t gridSize = nx * ny * nz;\n    \n    // Device grids (double buffering)\n    Real* d_grid1 = nullptr;\n    Real* d_grid2 = nullptr;\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaMalloc(&d_grid1, gridSize * sizeof(Real)));\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaMalloc(&d_grid2, gridSize * sizeof(Real)));\n\n    // Initialize\n    printf(\"Initializing grid...\\n\");\n    {\n        int device = 0, numSMs = 0;\n        CUDA_CHECK(cudaGetDevice(&device));\n        CUDA_CHECK(cudaDeviceGetAttribute(&numSMs, cudaDevAttrMultiProcessorCount, device));\n        const int threads = 256;\n        size_t blocks = (gridSize + threads - 1) / threads;\n        blocks = std::min<size_t>(blocks, (size_t)numSMs * 32);\n        if (blocks > 0) {\n            initializeGridKernel<<<(unsigned)blocks, threads>>>(d_grid1, gridSize);\n            CUDA_CHECK(cudaGetLastError());\n            // Both buffers share the (never changing) boundary values.\n            CUDA_CHECK(cudaMemcpy(d_grid2, d_grid1, gridSize * sizeof(Real), cudaMemcpyDeviceToDevice));\n        }\n        CUDA_CHECK(cudaDeviceSynchronize());\n    }\n\n    const bool hasInterior = nx > 2 && ny > 2 && nz > 2;\n    const dim3 block(BLOCK_X, BLOCK_Y, 1);\n    const dim3 grid(hasInterior ? All matched exactly.\n\n**Changes:**\n- **`CMakeLists.txt`:** `stencil3d.cpp` is now compiled as CUDA for the GPU it's built on. It deliberately doesn't use fast-math, so results stay bit-identical. The executable name is unchanged.\n- **Grid setup:** both grids are created and initialized on the GPU. The boundary values never change, so they're written into both buffers once at the start and the kernel only updates the interior. This gives the same result as copying the boundary every iteration.\n- **Stencil kernel:** each GPU thread handles one (x, y) column and steps through a section of z, keeping the cells above and below in registers. The neighbours are added in the same order as the original, so rounding is the same.\n- **Division by 7:** the 3090 is very slow at double-precision maths, and that, not memory, turned out to be the limit. Changing the block and section sizes made no difference (all about 19.6 GCUP/s). The IEEE `/ 7.0` is now done as a multiply by 1/7 plus one FMA correction step, which gives the correctly rounded result with fewer slow operations (about +30%). I checked it against real division on about 51 billion inputs, including all normal exponent ranges, with no mismatches. Very tiny (denormal) inputs weren't tested, but grid values in this program never get near that range. At about 9 double-precision instructions per cell, this is close to the most a single 3090 can do.\n- **Timing:** the timer still covers only the iteration loop, now with a GPU sync at the end so it measures finished work. The final grid is copied back to the CPU only for the existing `-r` and `-v` output.\n- A run with a negative odd iteration count still prints a zeroed second grid, as the original does.\n\nIt uses one GPU. The machine has four 3090s, and since the kernel is limited by double-precision speed, splitting the grid along z across them could give close to 4× more on large grids.
